Triggers: any mention of /mnt/user-data/uploads/, an uploaded_files section, a file_path tag, or a user asking about an uploaded file you have not yet read. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     6\t---\n     7\t\n     8\t# Reading Uploaded Files\n     9\t\n    10\t## Why this skill exists\n    11\t\n    12\tWhen a user uploads a file in claude.ai, Claude Desktop, or Cowork,\n    13\tthe file is written to `/mnt/user-data/uploads/<filename>` and you are told the path\n    14\tin an `<uploaded_files>` block. **The content is not in your context.**\n    15\tYou must go read it.\n    16\t\n    17\tThe naive thing \u2014 `cat /mnt/user-data/uploads/whatever` \u2014 is wrong for\n    18\tmost files:\n    19\t\n    20\t- On a PDF it prints binary garbage.\n    21\t- On a 100MB CSV it floods your context with rows you will never use.\n    22\t- On a DOCX it prints the raw ZIP bytes.\n    23\t- On an image it does nothing useful at all.\n    24\t\n    25\tThis skill tells you the right first move for each type, and when to\n    26\thand off to a deeper skill.\n    27\t\n    28\t## General protocol\n    29\t\n    30\t1. **Look at the extension.** That is your dispatch key.\n    31\t2. **Stat before you read.** Large files need sampling, not slurping.\n    32\t   ```bash\n    33\t   stat -c '%s bytes, %y' /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\n    34\t   file /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\n    35\t   ```\n    36\t3. **Read just enough to answer the user's question.** If they asked\n    37\t   \"how many rows are in this CSV\", don't load the whole thing into\n    38\t   pandas \u2014 `wc -l` gives a fast approximation (it counts newlines,\n    39\t   not CSV records, so it may over-count if quoted fields contain\n    40\t   embedded newlines).\n    41\t4. **If a dedicated skill exists, go read it.** The table below tells\n    42\t   you when. The dedicated skills cover editing, creating, and advanced\n    43\t   operations that this skill does not.\n    44\t\n    45\t## `extract-text`\n    46\t\n    47\tFor docx, odt, epub, xlsx, pptx, rtf, and ipynb the first move is\n    48\t`extract-text <file>`. It emits markdown for docx/odt/epub (headings,\n    49\tbold, lists, links, tables), tab-separated rows under `## Sheet:`\n    50\theaders for xlsx, text under `## Slide N` headers for pptx, fenced\n    51\tcode cells for ipynb, and plain text for rtf. Pass `--format <fmt>`\n    52\twhen the extension is wrong or absent (e.g., `--format xlsx` on an\n    53\t`.xlsm`). If it errors on a file, `pandoc <file> -t plain` is a\n    54\tfallback; for xlsx/pptx, fall back to the dedicated skill's\n    55\tPython-based approach (openpyxl / python-pptx).\n    56\t\n 93\t\n    94\tThen peek at the text content:\n    95\t\n    96\t```python\n    97\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader\n    98\tr = PdfReader(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\")\n When you need a structured preview in Python:\n   139\t\n   140\t```python\n   141\tfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n   142\twb = load_workbook(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.xlsx\", read_only=True)\n   143\tprint(\"Sheets:\", wb.sheetnames)\n   144\tws = wb.active\n   145\tfor row in ws.iter_rows(max_row=5, values_only=True):\n   146\t    print(row)\n   147\t```\n   148\t\n   149\t`read_only=True` matters \u2014 without it, openpyxl loads the entire\n   150\tworkbook into memory, which breaks on large files. Do not trust\n   151\t`ws.max_row` in read-only mode: many non-Excel writers omit the\n   152\tdimension record, so it comes back `None` or wrong. If you need a row\n   153\tcount, iterate or use pandas.\n   154\t\n   155\t**Legacy `.xls`** \u2014 openpyxl raises `InvalidFileException`. Use:\n   156\t\n   157\t```python\n   158\timport pandas as pd\n   159\tdf = pd.read_excel(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/old.xls\", engine=\"xlrd\", nrows=5)\n   160\t```\n   161\t\n   162\t**`.ods` (OpenDocument)** \u2014 openpyxl also rejects this. Use:\n   163\t\n   164\t```python\n   165\timport pandas as pd\n   166\tdf = pd.read_excel(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.ods\", engine=\"odf\", nrows=5)\n   167\t```\n   168\t\n   169\t---\n   170\t\n   171\t## PPTX\n   172\t\n   173\t```bash\n   174\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/deck.pptx | head -200\n   175\t```\n   176\t\n   177\t**Legacy `.ppt`** \u2014 convert to `.pptx` first via LibreOffice; see\n   178\t`/mnt/skills/public/pptx/SKILL.md` for the sandbox-safe\n   179\t`scripts/office/soffice.py` wrapper (bare `soffice` hangs here because\n   180\tthe seccomp filter blocks the `AF_UNIX` sockets LibreOffice uses for\n   181\tinstance management).\n   182\t\n   183\tFor anything beyond reading, go to `/mnt/skills/public/pptx/SKILL.md`.\n   184\t\n   185\t---\n   186\t\n   187\t## CSV / TSV\n   188\t\n   189\t**Do not** `cat` or `head` these blindly. A CSV with a 50KB quoted cell\n   190\tin row 1 will wreck your `head -5`. Use pandas with `nrows`:\n   191\t\n   192\t```python\n   193\timport pandas as pd\n   194\tdf = pd.read_csv(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\", nrows=5)\n   195\tprint(df)\n   196\tprint()\n   197\tprint(df.dtypes)\n   198\t```\n   199\t\n   200\tApproximate row count without loading (over-counts if the file has\n   201\tRFC-4180 quoted newlines \u2014 the same quoted-cell case this section\n   202\twarned about above):\n   203\t\n   204\t```bash\n   205\twc -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\n   206\t```\n   207\t\n   208\tFull analysis only after you know the shape:\n   209\t\n   210\t```python\n   211\tdf = pd.read_csv(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\")\n   212\tprint(df.describe())\n   213\t```\n   214\t\n   215\tTSV: same, with `sep=\"\\t\"`.\n   216\t\n   217\t---\n   218\t\n   219\t## JSON / JSONL\n   220\t\n   221\tStructure first, content second:\n   222\t\n   223\t```bash\n   224\tjq 'type' /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json\n   225\tjq 'if type == \"array\" then length elif type == \"object\" then keys else . end' /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json\n   226\t```\n   227\t\n   228\t(`keys` errors on scalar JSON roots \u2014 a bare `\"hello\"` or `42` is valid\n   229\tJSON per RFC 7159 \u2014 so guard the branch.)\n   230\t\n   231\tThen drill into what the user actually asked about.\n   232\t\n   233\tJSONL (one object per line) \u2014 do **not** `jq` the whole file; work line\n   234\tby line:\n   235\t\n   236\t```bash\n   237\thead -3 /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.jsonl | jq .\n   238\twc -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.jsonl\n   239\t```\n   240\t\n   241\t---\n   242\t\n   243\t## Images (JPG / PNG / GIF / WEBP)\n   244\t\n   245\t**You can already see uploaded images.** They are injected into your\n   246\tcontext as vision inputs alongside the `<uploaded_files>` pointer. You\n   247\tdo not need to read them from disk to describe them.\n   248\t\n   249\tThe disk copy is only needed if you are going to **process** the image\n   250\tprogrammatically:\n   251\t\n   252\t```python\n   253\tfrom PIL import Image\n   254\timg = Image.open(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/photo.jpg\")\n   255\tprint(img.size, img.mode, img.format)\n   256\t```\n   257\t\n   258\tFor OCR on an image (text extraction, not description):\n   259\t\n   260\t```python\n   261\timport pytesseract\n   262\tprint(pytesseract.image_to_string(img))\n   263\t```\n   264\t\n   265\tNote: the client resizes images larger than 2000\u00d72000 down to that\n   266\tbound and re-encodes as JPEG before upload, so the disk copy may not\n   267\tbe the user's original bytes. For most processing this doesn't matter;\n   268\tif the user is asking about original-resolution pixel data, flag it.\n   269\t\n   270\t---\n   271\t\n   272\t## Archives (ZIP / TAR / TAR.GZ)\n   273\t\n   274\t**List first. Extract never \u2014 unless the user explicitly asks.**\n   275\tArchives can be huge, contain path traversal, or nest forever.\n   276\t\n   277\t```bash\n   278\tunzip -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.zip\n   279\ttar -tf /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.tar\n   280\t```\n   281\t\n   282\tGNU tar auto-detects compression \u2014 `tar -tf` works on `.tar`,\n   283\t`.tar.gz`, `.tar.bz2`, `.tar.xz` alike. Don't hard-code `-z`.\n   284\t\n   285\tIf the user wants one file from inside, extract just that one:\n   286\t\n   287\t```bash\n   288\tunzip -p /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.zip path/inside/file.txt\n   289\t```\n   290\t\n   291\t**Standalone `.gz`** (not a tar) compresses a single file \u2014 there is\n   292\tno manifest to list. Just peek at the decompressed content:\n   293\t\n   294\t```bash\n   295\tzcat /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json.gz | head -50\n   296\t```\n   297\t\n   298\t---\n   299\t\n   300\t## EPUB / ODT\n   301\t\n   302\t```bash\n   303\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/book.epub | head -200\n   304\t```\n   305\t\n   306\tFor long ebooks, pipe through `head` \u2014 you rarely need the whole thing\n   307\tto answer a question.\n   308\t\n   309\t---\n   310\t\n   311\t## RTF / IPYNB\n   312\t\n   313\t```bash\n   314\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/notes.rtf | head -200\n   315\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/notebook.ipynb | head -200\n   316\t```\n   317\t\n   318\t---\n   319\t\n   320\t## Plain text / code / logs\n   321\t\n   322\tCheck the size first:\n   323\t\n   324\t```bash\n   325\twc -c /mnt/user-data/uploads/app.log\n   326\t```\n   327\t\n   328\t- **Under ~20KB**: `cat` is fine.\n   329\t- **Over ~20KB**: `head -100` and `tail -100` to orient. Established in\nSession 23 in response to specific Claude failures observed that\nsession.\n\n---\n\n## RULE 1 \u2014 Single integrity-checked zip\n\nEvery handoff is delivered as ONE zip file containing:\n- All files from the previous handoff zip (preserved without loss)\n- This session's conversation transcript or narrative\n- All files produced this session\n\nBefore delivery, two checks must be run and reported to Ramalingam:\n\n**Integrity check:** every file from the previous handoff zip is\npresent in the new zip and uncorrupted. The\nhandoff ZIP preserves chronological history (Rule 3) so the next\nClaude can read the process linearly. These are different contexts.\n\n### 2b. Spec self-containment\n\nSpec files must include backlog items deferred during critique cycles\nINSIDE the spec, with full deferral rationale and trigger conditions.\nNot just a CHANGELOG one-liner; a dedicated section.\n\nPattern: see \u00a7 13.5 of `buildemup_S7a_SPEC_v1_0_LOCKED.txt`. weights_applied:          Mapping[str, float]   # post-renormalization\n    weights_raw:              Mapping[str, float]   # pre-normalization (\u00a7 14.16)\n    signal_dominance:         float                  # NEW v0.5 \u2014 max(weights_raw) / sum(weights_raw);\n                                                     # \u2248 0.33 = balanced; \u2248 1.0 = single signal dominates\n SIGNAL_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD\n    climate_profile:          str\n    rule_trace:               tuple[str, ...]\n    permutation_search_size:  int\n    chosen_over_seed:         bool\n    seed_distance:            int\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass OrientationPriority:\n    direction_priorities: Mapping[PlotOrientation, DirectionPriorityScore]\n    refined_zone_bands:   Mapping[ZoneBand, 2.0\n```\n\nAdjacency: `N \u2192 {NW, NE}`; `E \u2192 {NE, SE}`; `S \u2192 {SE, SW}`; `W \u2192 {SW, NW}`.\n\n### \u00a7 4.2 \u2014 Signal weighting + dominance derivation\n\n(weighting unchanged from       "code": "    # Edge case: all weights zero (e.g., OFF tier in climate that disables sun+wind?)\n    # Should never happen in v1 valid inputs; defensive handling.\n    signal_dominance = 0.0\n    dominant_signal = None\n```\n\n**Interpretation guide for consumers**:\n\n- `signal_dominance \u2248 0.33` (3 equal weights) \u2192 balanced; no single signal drives the orientation\n- `signal_dominance \u2248 0.45-0.55` \u2192 moderate dominance; one signal carries more weight but others contribute meaningfully\n- `signal_dominance \u2248 0.7+` \u2192 heavy dominance; one signal nearly determines the orientation\n- `dominant_signal == \"sun\"` \u2192 sun path is the primary driver (composite/temperate climates with OFF tier)\n- `dominant_signal == \"wind\"` \u2192 wind/cross-ventilation is the primary driver (warm-humid with OFF tier)\n- `dominant_signal == \"vastu\"` \u2192 Vastu is the primary driver (FULL tier in mild climate zones)\n- `dominant_signal == None` \u2192 balanced; the optimization integrates multiple signals roughly equally\n\n### \u00a7 4.3 \u2014 Per-direction per-function score computation\n\n(unchanged from v0.4)\n\n```\nfunction_scores[direction][f] = (\n    sun_score(direction)         * sun_function_lookup(f)  * w_sun\n  + wind_score(direction, clim)  * wind_function_lookup(f) * w_wind\n  + vastu_score_4dir(direction, f, tier)                   * w_vastu\n)\n```\n\n`sun_function_lookup`: LIVING=1.0, BEDROOM=0.7, KITCHEN=0.6, POOJA=0.8, WET_AREA=0.3, UTILITY=0.3\n`wind_function_lookup`: LIVING=0.9, BEDROOM=1.0, KITCHEN=0.6, POOJA=0.5, WET_AREA=0.4, UTILITY=0.3\n\n### \u00a7 4.4 \u2014 Refining zone_bands\n\n(unchanged from v0.4)\n\n**Step 1**: enumerate all band \u2192 direction permutations (\u2264 4! = 24 non-COURTYARD; \u2264 4^4 = 256 COURTYARD).\n\n**Step 2**: prune by entry-on-road (PUBLIC must face plot.facing or secondary_road_direction on corner plots) + assert `len(surviving_perms) <= MAX_PERMUTATION_COUNT (= 256)` defense-in-depth.\n\n**Step 3**: score every surviving permutation.\n\n```\ntotal_score(perm) = (\n    \u03a3_(b \u2208 functional_bands) function_scores[band_to_function[b]][perm[b]]\n  + 0.5 \u00d7 function_scores[CIRCULATION][perm[CIRCULATION]]\n)\n\nfunction_scores[CIRCULATION][direction] = 0.3\u00d7sun + 0.2\u00d7wind + 0.5\n```\n\n**Step 4**: apply hysteresis (`SWAP_HYSTERESIS_THRESHOLD = 0.10`) vs. C5 seed; tie-break with 5-tier `tie_break_key`:\n\n1. -total_score\n2. hamming_distance(perm, seed_perm)\n3. -function_scores[LIVING][perm[PUBLIC]]\n4. -function_scores[BEDROOM][perm[PRIVATE]]\n5. lex_band_direction(perm)\n\n**Step 5**: record `score_margin = best_score - second_best_score`.\n\n### \u00a7 4.5 \u2014 Confidence (margin-clamped)\n\n(unchanged from v0.4)\n\n```\nMIN_DENOM = 0.1\npriority_confidence = clamp(\n    (top_score - second_score) / max(top_score, MIN_DENOM),\n    0.0, 1.0\n)\n```\n\n### \u00a7 4.6 \u2014 Validator\n\n(unchanged from v0.4 + one v0.5 invariant)\n\n1. `direction_priorities` covers exactly N, E, S, W.\n2. Every `function_scores` value in [0, 1].\n3. `signal_breakdown.vastu_score == 0.0` iff `vastu_tier == OFF`.\n4. Refined `zone_bands` keeps the C5-required band set.\n5. Per S30's pattern I want all four resolved before writing a line of code (Pattern E avoidance).\n\n---\n\n**Q1 \u2014 STRUCTURAL \u2014 `PlotDirection8` vs existing `PlotOrientation` + intercardinal plot.facing handling.**\n\nThe spec \u00a7 3 introduces a new `PlotDirection8` enum and \u00a7 4.6 invariant 1 says *\"direction_priorities covers exactly N, E, S, W\"*. But:\n\n- `domain/envelope.py:PlotOrientation` is **already 8-direction** (N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW). The spec author appears to have assumed it was 4-dir \u2014 `PlotDirection8` would be a redundant duplicate.\n- C4 fixture `_c4_fixtures.py:36` has `facing=PlotOrientation.NORTHEAST`. `compute_plot_facing_sides()` is \"tested for all 8 PlotOrientation values.\"\n- C5's `default_zone_bands(facing)` therefore produces intercardinal zone_bands when facing is intercardinal \u2014 e.g. `{PUBLIC: NORTHEAST, SERVICE: SOUTHWEST, ...}`.\n- C6 spec has **no failure mode and no aggregation rule** for intercardinal `plot.facing`. Hamming distance and seed-comparison will silently break.\n\nThree resolution paths \u2014 I have no confident pick:\n\n- **(a) Cardinal-only v1, intercardinal raises NotImplementedError** (new failure mode + new B-NNN). Cleanest. Defers a real product question.\n- **(b) Coerce intercardinal facing to nearest cardinal** at C6's input boundary (e.g. NE \u2192 either N or E by some tiebreak rule). Lossy; spec needs the rule explicitly.\n- **(c) Extend C6 scoring tables to 8-dir externally** (sun/wind tables get NE/SE/SW/NW rows). Bigger spec amendment; v0.5 is locked.\n\nEither way, **`PlotDirection8` should drop**: it duplicates `PlotOrientation`. The spec wording was based on a wrong premise about the existing enum.\n\n**Recommendation: (a)** \u2014 minimal blast radius, file new B-NNN for \"intercardinal plot.facing support\" (B-107), match the pattern of B-066/B-098. But it's your call.\n\n---\n\n**Q2 \u2014 DESIGN \u2014 `CIRCULATION` as pseudo-function.** Spec \u00a7 4.4 step 3 writes `function_scores[CIRCULATION][...]` but `function_scores: Mapping[FunctionRole, float]` and `FunctionRole` (\u00a7 3) does not include CIRCULATION. **Recommendation:** treat as a private `_circulation_score(direction, sun_score, wind_score) -> float` helper used inside the optimizer's total_score formula. Public `DirectionPriorityScore.function_scores` stays clean, keyed only by the 6 FunctionRole members. OK?\n\n---\n\n**Q3 \u2014 MODULE BOUNDARIES.** Mirroring C5's 5-file pattern (schema, decision_table, scorers, zone_bands, select), I propose for C6:\n\n- `schema.py` \u2014 enums + dataclasses + named constants (SIGNAL_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD, SWAP_HYSTERESIS_THRESHOLD, MAX_PERMUTATION_COUNT, MIN_DENOM)\n- `signals.py` \u2014 sun/wind/road/vastu score tables + weighting + signal_dominance derivation\n- `vastu_kb.py` \u2014 the 8-dir \u00d7 6-function PARTIAL Vastu table (isolated; FULL hard-fails here)\n- `optimizer.py` \u2014 permutation enum + entry-on-road prune + scoring + 5-tier tie-break + hysteresis + margin-clamped confidence\n- `select.py` \u2014 `prioritize_orientation` orchestrator\n- `__init__.py` \u2014 package re-exports\n\nAlternative is to inline the Vastu table inside `signals.py` (4 files instead of 5). I prefer the 5-file split because the Vastu table is tabular KB data and isolating it makes B-099's eventual FULL-tier expansion tidier.
